skill of the repairman.

                             Section II. REPAIRS

77. Replacement of Parts                             78. Dynamotor Inspection and Repair

   Most of the partsin the equipment are ac-              Note. Refer to paragraph 34 for lubrication in-
cessible and can be replaced without special in-     structions.
structions if they are found faulty. However,
observe the precautions given below:                     a. Inspect brushes, brush assemblies, brush
                                                     holders, and all miscellaneous parts after 50
   a. Tagging Leads. Tagging leads is essential      hours of operation, as follows:
to assure that correct rewiring will be made
when a part is replaced. Before unsoldering                  (1) Check and clean all accessible parts
leads from transformers, tube sockets, panel                      with a clean lint-free cloth.
connectors, or other parts, tie together the
leads that are attached to each of these parts.              (2) Inspect all machine screws and nuts
With small tags or short pieces of adhesive                         on all supports or mounting brackets
tape, identifayll wires in accordance with their                   and see that they are tight.
numbered connections. Identify every lead that
is to be removed.                                        b. Inspect brushes for defects and length;
                                                     see if they are broken, cracked, or chipped.
   b. Parts and Substitution. When damaged
parts must be replaced, identical parts should          c. Inspect for loosely fastened or broken-off
be used. If identical parts are not available and    brush shunts (pigtails).
the damaged component is beyond repair, a
substitution must be made. The part substi-             d. Check for burned-off or broken strands
tuted must have identical electrical properties      (kinked, worn, or otherwise damaged) ; replace
and must be of equal or higher voltage and           with complete brush assemblies obtained from
current rating.                                      spares.

    c. Location. Relocation  or substitution of          Note. During replacement, comply with proper
parts may develop certain    difficulties such as    markings on brushes and brush holders (+ to +;
hum, noise, or crosstalk,    and is not recom-       — to-—). This is done for quick and proper replace-
mended.                                              ment, thereby eliminating a sand-in operation. No
                                                     sanding should be necessary to obtain a good brush fit
   d. Mounting. Mount the new or replaced            if it is replaced correctly.

part in the same mounting position as that              e. Inspect brushes for length. Replace the
formerly occupied by the damaged part. Fasten        brush if the brush spring extends less than
all mountings securely.                              14-inch out of the holder when the brush is
                                                     seated against the commutator.

    e. Soldering. Before soldering any connec-           f. Inspect both of the commutators for clean-
 tion, carefully scrape all parts that will be       liness, excessive wear, flats, or any unevenness.
 touched by the solder until all traces of rust,     ‘Replace a dynamotor having worn or otherwise
corrosion, and paint or varnish are removed.         damaged commutators with a new dynamotor.
Dust the sprayed parts with a small clean brush.
Clean all parts to be soldered. Wrap the wire        g. Check bearings for loose shaft fit and de-
around the solder lug so that it is mechanically
 secure. Use very little solder and sufficient heat  fects such as binding or excessive wear as noted
to make shielded leads secure; make sure that
the shield is soldered properly to the ground        by wobble between the inner and outer races.
lug. The same applies to bonded connections
between metal subassembly frames and the             Do not remove bearings from the shaft. Replace
chassis plate. Make sure that the ground lug
                                                     a dynamotor having defective or worn bearings
is bonded securely to the chassis. Be sure to
clean away all particles or splashes of solder.      with a new dynamotor.

                                                     Note. The above operation is to be performed only

        SHIPMENT AND LIMITED STORAGE AND DEMOLITION

95. Repacking for Shipment or Limited              Demolition of the  equipment will be accom-
          Storage                                  plished only upon  order of the commanding

   a. ‘The exact procedure in repacking for ship-
ment or limited storage depends on the material    97. Methods of Destruction
available and the conditions under which the

given.                                             headsets, using sledges, axes handaxes, pick-
                                                   axes, hammers, crowbars, vr heavy tools
   b. Whenever practicable, place a dehydrating
agent such as silica gel inside the chests. Pro-       o. Cut. Cut cords, headsets, ana wiring,
tect the chests with a waterproof paper barrier.   using axes, handaxes, or machetes.
Seal the seams of the paper barrier with water-
proof sealing compound or tape. Pack the pro-         c. Burn. Burn cords, resistors, capacitors,
tected chests in a padded wooden case, providing   coils, wiring, and technical manuals, using gas-

the packing case.
